outputs are respectively 3,2,1,0; the adjoint restores every input.

One B5 record `class:A,width:2` lies about bit 1 and exceeds A's capacity;
class B has the symmetric problem. Two `mut:0` records lose uniqueness;
two new source slots change the interface and its hashes unnecessarily.

| Alternative | Tradeoff |
|---|---|
| Ordered per-bit class vector | Precise, but repeats one class name per bit. |
| Ordered maximal class fragments | Precise, compact for runs, retains the source slot. Recommended. |
| Require a homogeneous class or split the source signature | Removes this legal placement or changes the program interface. |

## Recommended exact spec diff

In §9.4 replace the `slots` record production with:

```text
{id, role, width, class} |
{id, role, width, fragments:[{offset, width, class}, ...]}
```

Append:

```text
Each source slot still has exactly one record, its original id and role,
and its complete signature width. Exactly one of class and fragments
is present. class abbreviates one fragment at offset 0 covering width.
For the fragmented form, fragment offsets and widths are nonnegative
and positive integers respectively, using §9.1 integer encodings.
Fragments are in increasing offset order, start at 0, meet without gaps
or overlap, and end at the instantiated slot width. Each class is a
resolved declared target class. Adjacent equal-class fragments must be
merged; use the class form when there is only one fragment.

Flatten bundle bits LSB first, or region cells in address order and
each cell LSB first, exactly as for the existing slot. A fragment names
the class of that half-open bit interval; it is not a new source slot,
allocation, lifetime, alias or permission to share storage. Repeated
slot ids, wrong roles/widths, gaps, overlaps or out-of-range fragments
reject. Slot widths retain §8.1's c-only expression rules; fragment
widths/offsets describe the resolved fixed signature width.

With topology, every bit's fragment class must equal the class of its
§11 vertex; vertices and simultaneous live uses remain disjoint. A
callee's fragments must equal the inherited operand-class vector. In
the absence of topology, they bind that resolved vector without naming
vertices. Capacity, occupancy and peak accounting use each bit's actual
class, and charge no wire twice. Existing homogeneous records and
scratch/history/borrow/ancilla schemas retain their meanings.
```

The witness descriptor is one slot
`{id:"mut:0",role:"mut",width:2,fragments:[{offset:0,width:1,class:"A"},`
`{offset:1,width:1,class:"B"}]}`. This is proposed record notation;
the production's field names are exact. It is not accepted B5 today.

## Hashes, catalog and consumers

The slot/signature/source closure stays the same. A repaired certificate
changes bytes; its existing placement schedule and target need not.
No reference recipe, leaf ordinal or catalog hash changes. Circuits,
scheduler, estimator and checker must validate fragments against the same
bit ordering and retain class-qualified resource rows. This does not
alter the explicit class of a source `qalloc` or add routing.

## Conformance vectors and verification

Test the four outputs and inverse above, both topology and class-only
descriptors, a region[1,2] slot with four flattened bits, and a homogeneous
legacy record. Reject a gap, overlap, width zero, split duplicate id,
incorrect class for a vertex, reversed offset order and adjacent unmerged
runs. Add a controlled invocation with the control charged separately,
not inserted into `mut:0`. The exact finite arithmetic is checked in
[INDEX](INDEX.md); target/scheduler implementation is not executed here.

Source context read: circuits' `docs/design/2026-11-01-m5-contract-requests.md`,
“Heterogeneous interface-slot frames”. Its named refusal is honest
incompleteness, not a new legality rule for source programs.

## Release and route

Recommend a **0.9 K/B5 encoding extension** alongside v2v; the gaps are
independent and both require vectors. Waiting for 0.10 leaves an explicit
blocker for complete 0.9 certificates, not permission to omit mixed
placements. Keep 4ta open for council and consumer work.

name: beads
description: Use when working in a repository that uses bd or Beads for durable project task tracking, issue dependencies, blocker management, multi-session handoff, or shared work memory. Trigger when the user asks to find ready work, claim or close tasks, create follow-up work, inspect blockers, recover project context, or choose between local planning and persistent project tracking.

# Beads

Use Beads as the shared project task system. Local plans, scratch files, and personal memories are useful, but they are not the durable source of truth for project work.

## First Step

Run:

```bash
bd prime
```

If that prints nothing, check whether the repository has an active Beads workspace:

```bash
bd where
```

## Preferred Route

Use the `bd` CLI when shell access is available. It is the most compact and direct Beads interface.

## Core CLI Workflow

1. Find work:

```bash
bd ready
bd list --status=open
bd list --status=in_progress
```

2. Inspect before editing:

```bash
bd show <id>
```

3. Claim work atomically:

```bash
bd update <id> --claim
```

4. Create durable follow-up work when implementation reveals new tasks:

```bash
bd create "Short title" --description="Why this exists and what needs to be done" --type=task --priority=2
```

5. Close completed work:

```bash
bd close <id> --reason="Completed"
```

## What Belongs In Beads

Use Beads for:

- shared project tasks
- blockers and dependencies
- discovered follow-up work
- work that must survive thread reset, compaction, or handoff
- status that another person or agent should be able to resume

Use agent-local planning tools only for the current turn's execution checklist. Do not treat them as shared project state.

## Rules

- Do not create markdown TODO files as the source of truth when Beads is available.
- Do not use `bd edit`; it opens an interactive editor. Use `bd update` flags instead.
- Prefer `--json` when parsing `bd` output programmatically.
- If hooks are installed, `bd prime` may already be injected. Run it manually when context is missing.
- Do not auto-close or mutate tasks unless the work is actually complete.

# 4ta — one interface slot placed across several classes

Council proposal for `b-isa-spec-4ta` (P1).

## Defect and North Star scope

ISA 0.9 §11 “Topology placement” supplies one vertex **per bit**, in
§9.4 slot order, and callees inherit actual operand placements. §8.3
assigns a class to each vertex. Neither clause requires all bits of a
source interface slot to have the same class. B5 (§9.4), however, gives
each slot exactly one `class` and its full signature `width`; unknown
fields and duplicate slot identities are not an extension mechanism
(§9.1). A legal mixed placement has no truthful descriptor. This is a
contract defect under C4/C5/C8, distinct from [v2v](b-isa-spec-v2v.md):
4ta concerns different bits **within one invocation of one slot**.

North Star §§4.1/4.3/4.4 promises virtual memory, allocation classes and
target-driven placement/resource reporting. Splitting a virtual slot
must not require a user rewrite or a target with a larger homogeneous
class. Class capacities still constrain physical feasibility; no capacity
is manufactured by this repair.

## Alternatives and worked witness

An action `split(t:wire[2] mut)` applies `x t[0]` and `x t[1]`.
A target has two vertices, 0:A and 1:B, each class capacity 1, and exact
single-wire X entries of duration 1 for either class. Placement
`interface_vertices:[[0,1]]` is legal: there is no multi-wire adjacency
requirement to satisfy. Both bits stay live for two sequential slots.
For unit class rates, time=2, peak[A]=peak[B]=1 and
qubit_second[A]=qubit_second[B]=2. On the four values t=0,1,2,3, the
outputs are respectively 3,2,1,0; the adjoint restores every input.

One B5 record `class:A,width:2` lies about bit 1 and exceeds A's capacity;
class B has the symmetric problem. Two `mut:0` records lose uniqueness;
two new source slots change the interface and its hashes unnecessarily.

| Alternative | Tradeoff |
|---|---|
| Ordered per-bit class vector | Precise, but repeats one class name per bit. |
| Ordered maximal class fragments | Precise, compact for runs, retains the source slot. Recommended. |
| Require a homogeneous class or split the source signature | Removes this legal placement or changes the program interface. |

## Recommended exact spec diff

```text

{id, role, width, fragments:[{offset, width, class}, ...]}
```

Append:

```text

and its complete signature width. Exactly one of class and fragments
is present. class abbreviates one fragment at offset 0 covering width.
For the fragmented form, fragment offsets and widths are nonnegative
and positive integers respectively, using §9.1 integer encodings.
Fragments are in increasing offset order, start at 0, meet without gaps
or overlap, and end at the instantiated slot width. Each class is a
resolved declared target class. Adjacent equal-class fragments must be
merged; use the class form when there is only one fragment.

Flatten bundle bits LSB first, or region cells in address order and
each cell LSB first, exactly as for the existing slot. A fragment names
the class of that half-open bit interval; it is not a new source slot,
allocation, lifetime, alias or permission to share storage. Repeated
slot ids, wrong roles/widths, gaps, overlaps or out-of-range fragments
reject. Slot widths retain §8.1's c-only expression rules; fragment

`{id:"mut:0",role:"mut",width:2,fragments:[{offset:0,width:1,class:"A"},`
`{offset:1,width:1,class:"B"}]}`. This is proposed record notation;

# Contract pass 2 — council decision index and verification

These are proposals and dispositions, not spec edits or implementation
acceptance. All 13 requested beads were read with `bd show`. ISA records
were read in their originating checkout. Dialect `bd show` could not open
the sibling's read-only Dolt LOCK, including with `--readonly`; copying its
live embedded database to job scratch allowed both `bd --readonly show`
commands to succeed without writing the source repository. The passive
JSONL export was not imported or used as the authoritative database.

The binding COMMON/CLAUDE rules, all first-pass contract notes and the
historical K82 note were read before derivation. Citations below refer to
ISA `spec/Bennett-ISA-Spec-0.9.md`, both maintained dialect specifications
under `../bennett-dialect-spec/spec/`, North Star
`../bennett/docs/Bennett-NorthStar.md`, and the council's ISA rubric.
Originating requests, source fixtures and selected implementation boundaries
were inspected read-only as each note identifies.

| Bead | Verdict | Recommended route |
|---|---|---|
| [b-isa-spec-0jq](b-isa-spec-0jq.md) | Structural-only applicability and action reference hash are gaps; qinplace owner already specified | 0.9 K; null/no-census semantics and linked owner-record checks |
| [b-isa-spec-4ta](b-isa-spec-4ta.md) | One legal mixed-class slot has no B5 descriptor | 0.9 K/B5 class fragments; preserve slot identity |
| [b-isa-spec-v2v](b-isa-spec-v2v.md) | One closure's frames can differ across equal variant/control contexts | 0.9 K; exact context-indexed frames with uniform shorthand |
| [b-isa-spec-7s0](b-isa-spec-7s0.md) | Existing restricted D_F proof has no frame/workspace claim binding | 0.9 K; selected-frame tensor relation, exact K76/K78 entry domain |
| [b-isa-spec-95w](b-isa-spec-95w.md) | Already covered native-model gap; add exact-entry/context/digest qualifications | First-pass 0.9 K plus versioned model; if 0.10, retain explicit current blocker |
| [b-isa-spec-0n5](b-isa-spec-0n5.md) | No demonstrated semantic defect; local diagnostic/report API improvement | First-pass optional editorial/report guidance; no new K requirement |
| [b-isa-spec-jw7](b-isa-spec-jw7.md) | Already covered missing fault census invocation | First-pass 0.9 K; coordinate action applicability and common context set |
| [b-isa-spec-cwq](b-isa-spec-cwq.md) | Already covered repeated-context resource ambiguity | First-pass 0.9 K; align frames/class rows and prevent overlapping-view double counting |
| [b-isa-spec-ysw](b-isa-spec-ysw.md) | Tuple-only bindings lose observable token equality | 0.9 K plus additive/versioned ABI; preserve every consistent alias partition |
| [b-isa-spec-552](b-isa-spec-552.md) | Current published-0.9 fixture admits B5/K13; old boundary is migration/tool evidence | Existing 0.9 adoption and producer/reference follow-up, no new spec mechanism |
| [b-isa-spec-ojy](b-isa-spec-ojy.md) | 31/54 fixture interface arrays contradict K82 body order | Repair fixture under existing K82; no catalog rewrite/new K |
| [b-dialect-spec-2cg](b-dialect-spec-2cg.md) | Minimal-wrapper versus production-history ambiguity; ordinary metadata mechanism exists | Current dialect §17.1 clarification in both maps; retain exact primitive trace |
| [b-dialect-spec-djm](b-dialect-spec-djm.md) | Process state already required; helper name is optional ABI convenience | Optional current dialect §19.2 helper contract; no ISA blocker |

No capability in the North Star thesis, §§3–5 or §8 ladder is removed.
In particular all unmodified SQLite, Julia, operating-system, Grover,
kernel-fuzzing and Monte Carlo requirements remain. An unchecked report
or a mechanics deferral records remaining work; it does not complete or
reduce that scope. No original issue is closed: council decisions and
consumer implementations remain with the listed beads. These findings
are already tracked; no duplicate or cross-repo tracker mutation is needed.

## Publication and combined schema decisions

“0.9 K repair” means the existing council-reviewed repair track, not
permission to alter a published semantic contract silently. `spec/README.md`
normally requires a full-version bump for semantic changes. Council must
choose that publication route and explicit subformat/ABI compatibility
rules. Every note's unchanged-source-hash statement assumes an authorized
in-version repair with unchanged source envelope bytes. A version bump
changes all affected version-prefixed envelopes and consumer pins. The
ref catalog changes only if its own versioned bytes change; none of these
proposals needs a silent rewrite of `ref@0.9`.

Use one context vocabulary across v2v/cwq/jw7: `context_key` is exactly
§12.1's concrete invocation chain in the bound root realisation, including
instance, call_index, loop iterations and applicable oracle step/phase.
The root key is []; nested default-covered calls are included. Resolve
variant/controls/arguments/budget/placements from that tree. Order arrays
by the unsigned UTF-8 bytes of each key's JCS encoding. Frame/resource/
fault arrays validate the same context identities where applicable;
variant/control-count alone is never an invocation identifier. K12 exact
rationals and outward resource display remain unchanged. ysw separately
adds the missing token equality relation; it does not change params_hash
or serialize process ids.

Recommended acceptance groups are applicability/owner binding (0jq),
frames and reports (4ta/v2v/cwq/jw7), borrow proofs (7s0), token context
(ysw), and native models (95w). The fixture and migration dispositions
should proceed with their existing owners. K numbers are assigned only
at acceptance, avoiding collisions with concurrent council work.

This code checks exact small mathematical witnesses and actual published
reference interfaces. The arithmetic is not an implementation of any new
certificate verifier or a substitute for its required conformance tests.

```python
from pathlib import Path
from itertools import product
from fractions import Fraction as F
import copy
import hashlib
import json
import os
import re
import subprocess

ROOT = Path('/home/tobias/Projects/bennett-workspace/bennett-isa-spec')
OUT = ROOT / 'docs/notes/2026-11-02-pass2'
SCRATCH = ROOT / '.tmp/sol-contract-pass2'
REF = SCRATCH / 'target/debug/bennett-isa-ref'
PROBE = SCRATCH / 'target/debug/k82-probe'
assert Path.cwd() == ROOT
assert os.environ['TMPDIR'] == str(SCRATCH / 'tmp')
assert os.environ['CARGO_TARGET_DIR'] == str(SCRATCH / 'target')
assert not any(k in os.environ for k in
               ('DISPLAY', 'WAYLAND_DISPLAY', 'DBUS_SESSION_BUS_ADDRESS', 'BROWSER'))

isa = ['0jq', '4ta', 'v2v', '7s0', '95w', '0n5', 'jw7', 'cwq',
       'ysw', '552', 'ojy']
ids = ['b-isa-spec-' + x for x in isa] + [
       'b-dialect-spec-2cg', 'b-dialect-spec-djm']
assert {p.name for p in OUT.iterdir()} == {x + '.md' for x in ids} | {'INDEX.md'}
for ident in ids:
    p = OUT / (ident + '.md')
    s = p.read_text()
    for title in ('Defect', 'North Star', 'Alternatives',
                  'Recommended exact spec diff', 'Hashes', 'Conformance', 'Release'):
        assert title.lower() in s.lower(), (ident, title)
    assert ident in s and not re.search(r'\b(?:TODO|TBD)\b', s)
for p in OUT.glob('*.md'):
    s = p.read_text()
    assert sum(line.startswith('```') for line in s.splitlines()) % 2 == 0, p
    # Remove fenced examples before treating Markdown syntax as links.
    prose = re.sub(r'```[^\n]*\n.*?\n```', '', s, flags=re.S)
    for link in re.findall(r'\[[^\]]+\]\(([^)]+)\)', prose):
        if not link.startswith(('http://', 'https://', '#')):
            assert (p.parent / link.split('#')[0]).exists(), (p, link)
index = (OUT / 'INDEX.md').read_text()
for ident in ids:
    assert index.count('| [' + ident + ']') == 1, ident
print('PASS: 13 per-bead notes and index; required sections, fences and links')

# 0jq: H = M/sqrt(2), so M*M=2I exactly; each H column has two nonzeros.
M = ((1, 1), (1, -1))
assert [[sum(M[i][k]*M[k][j] for k in range(2)) for j in range(2)]
        for i in range(2)] == [[2, 0], [0, 2]]
assert all(sum(M[i][j] != 0 for i in range(2)) == 2 for j in range(2))
# 95w: Bell +/- have norm squared 2 before division by sqrt(2), overlap zero.
plus, minus = [1, 0, 0, 1], [1, 0, 0, -1]
assert sum(a*b for a, b in zip(plus, minus)) == 0
assert sum(a*a for a in plus) == sum(a*a for a in minus) == 2
assert F(1)-F(1) == 0

# 4ta, v2v, cwq: exact basis maps and separate root/child footprints.
for t in range(4):
    assert (t ^ 3) ^ 3 == t
for t, u in product(range(2), repeat=2):
    assert ((t ^ 1) ^ 1, (u ^ 1) ^ 1) == (t, u)
assert (2, 1*2, 1*2, 1, 1) == (2, 2, 2, 1, 1)  # split time, A/B occupancy/peak
child_footprints = [(1, 1, 1), (1, 2, 2)]  # time, occupancy, peak
root = (2, 2*2, 2)
assert root == (2, 4, 2)
assert sum(c[2] for c in child_footprints) != root[2]
assert root[1] + sum(c[1] for c in child_footprints) != root[1]
assert F(1) + F(1,4) == F(5,4) and F(2) + F(1,4) == F(9,4)

# 7s0: a finite model of K76's all-operand stack rule for these CX-only prefixes.
def entry_c(sequence):
    values = {'a': None, 'x': None, 'c': 0}
    stacks = {w: [] for w in values}
    events = []
    for control, target in sequence:  # cx and mcx(1) already normalized to one kind
        identity = (control, target)
        operands = (control, target)
        top = stacks[control][-1] if stacks[control] else None
        if (top is not None and all(stacks[w] and stacks[w][-1] == top
                                   for w in operands)
                and events[top][0] == identity):
            before = events[top][1]
            for w in operands:
                stacks[w].pop()
                values[w] = before[w]
        elif values[control] == 0:
            continue  # disabled: no causal event
        else:
            event = len(events)
            events.append((identity, {w: values[w] for w in operands}))
            for w in operands:
                stacks[w].append(event)
            values[target] = (values[target] ^ values[control]
                              if all(values[w] is not None for w in operands)
                              else None)
    return values['c']
assert entry_c([]) == 0
assert entry_c([('a','c'), ('a','c')]) == 0
assert entry_c([('a','c'), ('x','c'), ('a','c'), ('x','c')]) is None
def frame_ok(c_values):
    return all((c, b ^ c) == (c, b) for c in c_values for b in range(2))
assert frame_ok([0]) and not frame_ok([0, 1])
assert not frame_ok(sorted(set([0] + [1])))  # repeated-visit D_F union cannot be narrowed
entry_products = [(0,0), (1,1)]
assert all(b ^ c ^ d == b for c,d in entry_products for b in range(2))
assert not all(b ^ c ^ d == b for c,d,b in product(range(2), repeat=3))
assert sum(a*b for a, b in zip((1,1), (1,-1))) == 0  # phase on B changes |+>

# 0n5: legal state size versus sample count, without allocating it.
assert (1 << 63) // 8 == (1 << 60)
# jw7: all input/target/fault tuples; F_B's code, not final XOR word.
for budget, expected_faults in [(1, 256), (2, 0)]:
    faults = 0
    for x, target, fault in product(range(4), range(4), range(16)):
        payload, code = (0, 1) if budget == 1 else ((x+1) % 4, 0)
        after = (x, target ^ payload, fault ^ code)
        assert 0 <= after[1] < 4 and 0 <= after[2] < 16
        faults += code != 0
    assert faults == expected_faults
assert 1 ^ 1 == 0 and 1 ^ 0 == 1

# ysw: identity partitions are independent of canonical tuple values.
def partition(handles):
    seen, result = {}, []
    for h in handles:
        if h not in seen:
            seen[h] = len(seen)
        result.append(seen[h])
    return result
def valid_labels(labels):
    return (all(type(x) is int and x >= 0 for x in labels)
            and labels == partition(labels))
assert partition([10,20,20]) == [0,1,1]
assert partition([10,20,30]) == [0,1,2]
assert partition([20,20]) == [0,0] and partition([]) == []
assert all(not valid_labels(x) for x in ([1,1], [0,2], [0,-1]))
args = [['1','0','0','0'], ['1','0','0','0']]
def jcs(x):
    return json.dumps(x, sort_keys=True, separators=(',', ':'), ensure_ascii=False).encode()
params = hashlib.sha256(jcs(args)).hexdigest()
assert params == hashlib.sha256(jcs(copy.deepcopy(args))).hexdigest()
assert jcs({'args':args, 'token_aliases':[0,0]}) != jcs(
           {'args':args, 'token_aliases':[0,1]})
assert (4 if partition([20,20]) == [0,0] else 3) == 4
assert (4 if partition([20,30]) == [0,0] else 3) == 3

# 2cg: exact primitive request/bump history; production metadata stores are not simulated.
def allocation_trace(production, limit):
    bump, records = 4096, []
    def alloc(n):
        nonlocal bump
        if bump + max(n, 1) > 4096 + limit:
            raise OverflowError
        p = bump
        records.append((p, n))
        bump += max(n, 1)
        return p
    payload, code = None, 0
    try:
        alloc(3)
        if production:
            alloc(0)
        padding = (-bump) % 16
        if padding:
            alloc(padding)
        payload = alloc(16)
        if production:
            alloc(32)
    except OverflowError:
        code = 2
    return payload, bump, records, code
minimal = allocation_trace(False, 32)
production = allocation_trace(True, 64)
assert minimal == (4112, 4128, [(4096,3),(4099,13),(4112,16)], 0)
assert production == (4112, 4160,
                      [(4096,3),(4099,0),(4100,12),(4112,16),(4128,32)], 0)
failed = allocation_trace(True, 32)
assert failed == (4112, 4128, production[2][:-1], 2)
assert 16+32 == 32+16 == 48 and min(16,48) != min(32,48)
# djm: required public name/environment examples, not a musl process simulation.
assert '/bin/demo'.rsplit('/',1)[-1] == 'demo'
assert '/bin/'.rsplit('/',1)[-1] == ''
assert dict(s.split('=',1) for s in ['K=V'])['K'] == 'V'
print('PASS: structural, frame/resource, borrow, census, token, allocation and process witnesses')

def run(argv):
    p = subprocess.run([str(x) for x in argv], cwd=ROOT, text=True,
                       capture_output=True, timeout=120)
    assert p.returncode == 0, (argv, p.returncode, p.stdout, p.stderr)
    return p.stdout
fixture = ROOT / 'conformance/fixtures/controlled-action-09/controlled.bisa'

print('PASS: published B5/K13 check/hash identity and 4 forward/4 adjoint action cases')

# ojy: independent written-order sweep of the current 54 bare-formal recipes.
catalog_path = ROOT / 'spec/ref-0.9.json'
raw = catalog_path.read_bytes()
recipes = {r['kind']:r for r in json.loads(raw)['recipes']}
pin_ref = '5de4e4995350313e50217880ba1b95de39cd043283b726337d4894efb005d197'
assert hashlib.sha256(b'isa=0.9\nexpansion=ref\nversion=0.9\n'+raw+b'\n').hexdigest() == pin_ref
body_interfaces = {}
for kind, recipe in recipes.items():
    if not all(isinstance(p,str) for p in recipe['parameters']):
        continue
    used = []
    def walk(body, bound):
        for node in body:
            if 'gate' in node:
                for wire in node['wires']:
                    name = wire.split('[',1)[0]
                    if name not in bound and name not in used:
                        used.append(name)
            elif 'alloc' in node:
                bound.add(node['alloc'])
            elif 'for' in node:
                walk(node['body'], bound | {node['for']})
            elif 'inverse' in node:
                walk(node['body'], bound)
            elif 'release' in node:
                assert node['release'] in bound
            else:
                raise AssertionError((kind, node))  # no unexamined constructors
    walk(recipe['body'], set(recipe['parameters']))
    body_interfaces[kind] = used
assert len(body_interfaces) == 54
changed = [k for k,v in body_interfaces.items() if v != sorted(v)]
assert len(changed) == 31
assert body_interfaces['value.select'] == ['p','b','a','t']

# Execute the existing generator only through document construction, before its file-write branch.
# The one-line correction is in memory; the published generator/fixture are never written.
generator = ROOT / 'conformance/fixtures/accepted-k82-09/generate.py'
source = generator.read_text().split('target = HERE / "k82.json"',1)[0]
assert source.count('return sorted(used)') == 1
scope = {'__file__':str(generator), '__name__':'pass2_in_memory_generator'}
exec(compile(source.replace('return sorted(used)', 'return used'), str(generator), 'exec'), scope)
corrected = scope['doc']
original_path = generator.parent / 'k82.json'
original = json.loads(original_path.read_text())
assert corrected['interfaces'] == body_interfaces
assert sum(original['interfaces'][k] != corrected['interfaces'][k]
           for k in body_interfaces) == 31
changed_edges = sum(a['interface'] != b['interface']
                    for a,b in zip(original['edges'], corrected['edges']))
assert len(original['edges']) == len(corrected['edges']) == 20
assert changed_edges == 10
without_enumeration = copy.deepcopy(corrected)
without_enumeration['interfaces'] = original['interfaces']
for old, new in zip(original['edges'], without_enumeration['edges']):
    new['interface'] = old['interface']
assert without_enumeration == original
assert sum(len(e['instances']) for e in corrected['edges']) == 86
assert len(corrected['cases']) == 16
corrected_path = SCRATCH / 'k82-body-order.json'
corrected_path.write_bytes(jcs(corrected) + b'\n')
for path in (original_path, corrected_path):
    output = run([PROBE, path, catalog_path])
    assert 'edges: 86 match, 0 differ' in output
    assert sum(': PASS' in line for line in output.splitlines()) == 16, output
print('PASS: K82 31/54 ordering mismatches;', changed_edges,
      'edge interface arrays differ; all other fields identical; both probes 86/86 and 16/16')
print('K82 original fixture SHA-256:', hashlib.sha256(original_path.read_bytes()).hexdigest())
print('K82 corrected scratch fixture SHA-256:', hashlib.sha256(corrected_path.read_bytes()).hexdigest())
print('PASS: pass2 verification complete; proposed schemas remain unimplemented/unaccepted')

## Remaining acceptance work

Council must decide and publish the exact repairs above before consumers
invent fields. Implementation owners then add the proposed positive and
negative vectors to suites C/F/H and relevant checker/producer tests.
552 needs actual producer adoption; ojy needs the fixture-owner repair;
the two dialect dispositions belong to that contract's council. Every
bead stays open for those decisions/work. This notes-only pass is complete
when the listed commands and finite checks pass; it does not close the
underlying contract or implementation obligations.
# v2v — frame descriptors for every realised invocation context

Council proposal for `b-isa-spec-v2v` (P1).

## Defect and North Star scope

ISA 0.9 §12.1 “Invocation contexts” includes every reached invocation,
including defaults, with resolved arguments, budget and choices. §12.3
stores one region record per closure hash and requires one B5 frame.
§9.4 requires each slot's concrete target class and substitutes parameters
and budget into width constants before forming c-only expressions.
The same callee can receive A in one call and B in another; no single
descriptor can express both. A `(variant,controls)` key alone also fails
when those values agree. This is a C4/C5 contract defect.

North Star §§4.2/4.4/5.1 requires freely nested regions and independently
checkable schedules/certificates. Refusing repeated mixed-class calls
would narrow this machine. [4ta](b-isa-spec-4ta.md) describes a different
case: a single slot fragmented within one context. Both repairs compose.

## Alternatives and worked witness

Use the source from `../bennett-sched/tests/pipeline.rs`, test
`context_varying_frame_classes_are_explicitly_deferred_instead_of_misreported`:
root `pair(t,u)` calls the same one-bit `flip` on t and then u; flip is X.
Place t in A and u in B, capacity 1 each. Each call is forward with zero
inherited controls and the same closure hash. Callee frame one has
`mut:0,class:A,width:1`; callee frame two has B instead.

For all four root basis inputs, the result is `(t XOR 1,u XOR 1)`.
At unit duration/rate the root time is 2 and each root interface bit is
live for both slots: occupancy[A]=occupancy[B]=2, peaks 1 each.
The per-invocation callee views have duration 1 and just their respective
class. Neither copying A into both records nor taking a class wildcard
describes the realised placement. This witness needs no heterogeneous
slot and no nonzero epsilon.

| Alternative | Assessment |
|---|---|
| Duplicate region records or rename the callee | Breaks closure-keyed identity or unnecessarily changes the source. |
| Use one wildcard/maximal frame | Cannot verify concrete classes and actual widths. |
| Exact context-indexed frames, uniform shorthand retained | Describes both calls and keeps old uniform certificates. Recommended. |

## Recommended exact spec diff

Replace the §12.3 region `frame (required)` entry by:

```text
frame?: <§9.4 descriptor>,
frame_contexts?: [{context_key:<§12.1 key>, frame:<§9.4 descriptor>}]
```

Append:

```text
Each scheduled region record contains exactly one of frame and
frame_contexts. frame is a descriptor valid for every reached invocation
of that region in the bound root realisation, after instantiating c for
each invocation. It may be used only if all its concrete classes and
resolved non-c-dependent widths describe every invocation truthfully.
frame_contexts contains exactly one record for each reached invocation,
in increasing bytewise order of the JCS encoding of context_key. The
key is exactly §12.1's fully resolved invocation chain; no wildcard,
variant/control-only key, omitted default-covered call or repeated key
is admitted. The root invocation has key []. Resolve its variant,
controls, arguments, budget, placements and expansion choices from the
bound invocation tree, never from record order or a guessed root budget.

Select the context's descriptor before substituting c and check its
slots, classes, widths, allocation identities and lifetimes against
that invocation. Inherited controls remain caller-owned and separate
from callee slots. Distinct contexts have distinct dynamic frame
identities even when their static descriptor id strings agree. Identical
descriptor bytes do not authorize shared physical storage; §9.4's
live-range and disjointness rules remain required. A standalone reference
record uses frame for its explicitly bound invocation or a descriptor
proved uniform over its declared invocation scope.
```

Here `context_key` uses the same spelling as the first-pass resource and
fault proposals. Their arrays and `frame_contexts` must cover the same
reached context set when applicable, rather than enumerate independently.
The originating bead's field spelling `context` is superseded by this
consistent proposed spelling; neither is published yet.

## Hashes, catalog and consumers

No callee clone, source/interface/closure hash or catalog change is
required. Certificate bytes change. Existing schedules already distinguish
the two source call points; no new call edge is invented. Schedulers,
specialisers and checkers must retain full context keys across linked and
default-covered calls. Estimator frames and resource reports must select
the same context. A uniformly valid c-expression stays usable as `frame`;
the repair must not force needless duplication of uniform certificates.

## Conformance vectors and verification

Use the pair/flip case; check both class records and all four outputs,
then inverse restoration. Reject two A records, a missing default call,
duplicate/foreign key, both `frame` and `frame_contexts`, neither field,
and a width from another token budget at the same variant/control count.
Add a homogeneous two-call control accepted with old `frame`, and combine
one context with 4ta fragments. Finite output/resource arithmetic is
checked in [INDEX](INDEX.md). The sibling test was read, not run; its
current `Unsupported` expectation documents the gap, not a completed fix.

## Release and route

Recommend a **0.9 K certificate-context repair**, coordinated with
[cwq](b-isa-spec-cwq.md) and [jw7](b-isa-spec-jw7.md). Keep v2v open until
the encoding and consumer tests are accepted. A 0.10 deferral must retain
this explicit blocker; it cannot restrict the admissible call graph.

## Core Rules
- **Default**: Use beads for ALL task tracking (`bd create`, `bd ready`, `bd close`)
- **Prohibited**: Do NOT use TodoWrite, TaskCreate, or markdown files for task tracking
- **Workflow**: Create beads issue BEFORE writing code, mark in_progress when starting
- **Memory**: Use `bd remember "insight"` for persistent knowledge across sessions. Do NOT use MEMORY.md files — they fragment across accounts. Search with `bd memories <keyword>`.
- Persistence you don't need beats lost context
- Default: do not commit, push, or run dolt remote sync without explicit authority. Team-maintainer behavior is opt-in and still subordinate to user/orchestrator instructions.
- Git workflow: conservative by default; commit/push only with explicit user/orchestrator or team-maintainer authority
- Session management: check `bd ready` for available work

## Essential Commands

### Finding Work
- `bd ready` - Show issues ready to work (no blockers)
- `bd list --status=open` - All open issues
- `bd list --status=in_progress` - Your active work
- `bd show <id>` - Detailed issue view with dependencies

### Creating & Updating
- `bd create --title="Summary of this issue" --description="Why this issue exists and what needs to be done" --type=task|bug|feature --priority=2` - New issue
  - Priority: 0-4 or P0-P4 (0=critical, 2=medium, 4=backlog). NOT "high"/"medium"/"low"
- `bd create ... --parent=<id>` - Hierarchical child (task under epic, subtask under task; inherits parent labels)
- `bd update <id> --claim` - Claim work
- `bd unclaim <id>` - Release stuck issue (agent crashed)
- `bd update <id> --assignee=username` - Assign to someone
- `bd update <id> --if-assignee=<expected> --assignee=<new>` - Atomic reassign: applies only if the assignee still matches (--if-status=<expected> guards status; --if-assignee='' requires unassigned). Mismatch exits non-zero with nothing written — never retry blindly
- `bd update <id> --title/--description/--notes/--design` - Update fields inline
- `bd close <id>` - Mark complete
- `bd close <id1> <id2> ...` - Close multiple issues at once (more efficient)
- `bd close <id> --reason="explanation"` - Close with reason
- **Tip**: When creating multiple issues/tasks/epics, use parallel subagents for efficiency
- **WARNING**: Do NOT use `bd edit` - it opens $EDITOR (vim/nano) which blocks agents

### Dependencies & Blocking
- `bd dep add <issue> <depends-on>` - Add dependency (issue depends on depends-on)
- `bd blocked` - Show all blocked issues
- `bd show <id>` - See what's blocking/blocked by this issue

### Sync & Collaboration
- `bd search <query>` - Search issues by keyword

### Project Health
- `bd stats` - Project statistics (open/closed/blocked counts)
- `bd doctor` - Check for issues (sync problems, missing hooks)
- `bd doctor --check=conventions` - Check for convention drift (lint, stale, orphans)

### Quality Tools
- `bd create --validate` - Check description has required sections
- `bd create --acceptance="criteria"` - Set acceptance criteria (checked by --validate)
- `bd create --design="decisions"` - Record design decisions
- `bd create --notes="context"` - Add supplementary notes
- `bd config set validation.on-create warn` - Auto-validate on every create
- `bd lint` - Check existing issues for missing sections

### Lifecycle & Hygiene
- `bd defer <id> --until="date"` - Defer work to a future date
- `bd supersede <id> --with=<new-id>` - Mark issue as superseded
- `bd close <id> --suggest-next` - Show newly unblocked issues after closing
- `bd stale` - Find issues with no recent activity
- `bd orphans` - Find issues with broken dependencies
- `bd preflight` - Pre-PR checks (lint, stale, orphans)
- `bd human <id>` - Flag for human decision (list/respond/dismiss)

### Structured Workflows
- `bd formula list` - See available workflow templates
- `bd mol pour <name>` - Start structured workflow from formula

## 8. Target descriptions (target 0.9)

### 8.1 Format and expressions

A target is a JSON document in RFC 8785 canonical form; `target_hash` =
SHA-256 of those bytes; numbers are exact rationals from their decimal
text. Cost expressions are a closed JSON AST:

```
  expr := number | {"var": v} | {"op": o, "args": [expr, …]} | {"table": name, "index": "k" | "eps_decade"}
  v    := "k" | "c" | "eps" | "a" | "w" | "i" | "theta"
  o    := "+" (2) | "-" (2) | "*" (2) | "/" (2) | "max" (2) | "min" (2) | "ceil" (1) | "log2inv_eps" (0)
```

`log2inv_eps` = the exact integer min{ j ≥ 0 : eps · 2^j ≥ 1 } for 0 <
eps ≤ 1; eps outside (0, 1] is a load-time error; an entry with
`epsilon` 0 MUST NOT reference `eps` or `log2inv_eps`. Division by zero
and a table index outside the array are load-time errors. Costs MUST be
nonnegative and finite.

**EPS-DECADE** (K9; source: bennett-vm-cxg). A table expression with
`index: "eps_decade"` evaluates its zero-based array index as the exact
integer min{ j ≥ 0 : eps · 10^j ≥ 1 }. It requires a context binding
`eps` and 0 < eps ≤ 1; otherwise it is a load-time error. Thus it uses
the requested `epsilon_share`, not the entry's achieved `epsilon`.
Evaluation uses exact rational comparisons, never a floating-point
logarithm. The existing out-of-array rule applies to this integer. An
entry with `epsilon` 0 MUST NOT use `eps_decade`, including through a
table lookup. This extends the exact-entry prohibition above; it does
not assign an index to eps = 0. Motivation (0.8 §8.1): the grammar names
`"index": "k" | "eps_decade"`, and "Division by zero and a table index
outside the array are load-time errors"; 0.8 defines no decimal index.

**EPS-DECADE witness**

```json
[{"eps":"1","index":0},{"eps":"0.1","index":1},{"eps":"0.1001","index":1},{"eps":"0.0999","index":2},{"eps":"0.001","index":3},{"eps":"0.000999","index":4}]
```

For eps = 999/10000, eps·10 = 999/1000 < 1 and eps·100 = 999/100 ≥ 1,
so the index is 2. A two-entry table rejects this index, a three-entry
table selects element 2. eps = 0, eps > 1 and an unbound eps reject;
no clamping, padding or fallback entry is admitted.

**Variable binding** by evaluation context; referencing a variable that
the context does not bind is a load-time error:

| Context | Bound variables |
|---|---|
| a `gates` entry's `epsilon` and `cost` | `k` = the gate's own control count; `c` = its total control count (`k` + effective control count, §8.3); `eps` = the operation's `epsilon_share` from the schedule (§11) |
| a scheme's `ancillae.count`, template wire subscripts and `repeat` bounds | `k`, `c` as above; `i` inside a `repeat`; `theta` for a parametric scheme |
| a template gate's `params` | `theta`, `i` |
| `qram` costs | `a` = the region's address width A; `w` = its cell width W; `c` |
| a frame descriptor (§9.4) | `c` only |
| `classes.*`, `capabilities.*`, `resources.*.rate` | none (numbers only) |

**Requested versus achieved accuracy.** `eps` is the `epsilon_share`
the schedule assigns to the operation (§11; the default share is the
region's `epsilon` divided by its number of ε > 0 operations). A `gates`
entry whose `epsilon` expression evaluates to a value > `eps` does not
match the operation and matching continues with the next entry; the
error charged to a matched operation is the entry's evaluated `epsilon`;
an entry with `epsilon` 0 is exact and matches every `eps`.

### 8.2 Resources, aggregation, timing

```
  "resources": { "<name>": { "kind": "count"|"time"|"occupancy"|"peak", "unit": "<string>", "rate"?: { "<cls>": n } } }
```

Mandatory: `qubit_second` (occupancy, per class), `time`, `peak_qubits`
(peak, per class). Aggregation from the **scheduled event list** (every
**leaf event** — a native gate after scheme expansion, a classical step,
an effect — with its start time and duration, every wire with its live
interval): `count` sums the per-event costs, each leaf exactly once (a
scheme contributes only its expanded template, never a cost of its own);
`time` is the makespan; `occupancy` integrates, per class, the number of
live wires times the class rate over time (a class absent from `rate`
has rate 1); `peak` is the maximum over time of the number of
concurrently live wires per class (concurrent demands sum). Classical
instructions and effects take `classical_step_time`. Sequential in
program order unless the schedule assigns start times; a gate occupies
its wires for its `time`; gates on disjoint wires MAY overlap unless
`max_parallel` is declared. Connectivity is all-to-all when `topology`
is absent, and is constrained by §8.3 when present (§17 item 15).
Interface wires are charged to the caller for the call's duration;
borrowed wires to the borrower at `dirty_rate`, replacing the owner's
charge.

### 8.3 Classes, gates, schemes, capabilities

```
  "ptr_widths_supported": [32, 64],
  "classes": { "<cls>": { "qubit_second": n, "dirty_rate": n, "capacity": n | "unbounded" } },
  "topology"?: { "vertices": [ { "class": "<cls>" }, … ], "edges": [ [u, v], … ] },
  "gates":   [ { "op": "<gate>", "classes": ["<cls>"|"*", …], "controls": {"min": n, "max": n | "unbounded"},
                 "params": ["theta"]?, "epsilon": expr, "cost": { "<resource>": expr, "time": expr } }, … ],
  "schemes": [ { "name": "<name>", "realises": { "op": "<gate>", "controls": {"min": n, "max": n | "unbounded"}, "params": ["theta"]? },
                 "ancillae": { "count": expr, "state": "clean" | "dirty", "class": "<cls>" },
                 "template": [ { "gate": "<op>", "wires": [ "c[<expr>]" | "t" | "t2" | "a[<expr>]", … ], "params": [expr]?,
                                 "repeat": { "var": "i", "from": expr, "to": expr }? }, … ] }, … ],
                 /* from inclusive, to exclusive; a scheme has no cost or epsilon of its own (§8.2) */
  "qram": null | { "load": { "cost": {…} }, "store": { "cost": {…} }, "exch": { "cost": {…} } },
  "capabilities": { "measurement": {"mid_circuit": bool, "feedforward_latency": n}, "mbu": false,     /* reserved, §6.4 */
                    "max_parallel": n | "unbounded", "max_predicate_depth": n | "unbounded", "classical_step_time": n },
  "objective": { "weighted": { "<resource>": n } } | { "lexicographic": [ "<resource>", … ] },
  "tables": { "<name>": [ n, … ] },
  "imports": { "<model>": { "version": "...", "map": { "<quantity>": ["<resource>", factor] } } }
```

**Matching.** An executed gate with opcode o, operand classes, **total
control count c** (own controls + effective control count) and
parameters matches the first `gates` entry in array order whose `op`,
class pattern and inclusive `controls` range cover it and whose
`epsilon` ≤ `eps` (§8.1); else the first `schemes` entry whose
`realises` covers it; else load-time error. Template wires: `c[e]` the
e-th control (0-based, e an expr over k, c and i) in the operation's
control order (own controls in operand order, then the effective control
context in predicate-stack order, outermost first), `t` the target (`t`
and `t2` the two wires of `swap`/`cz` in operand order), `a[e]` the e-th
ancilla; `params` expressions may use `theta`; `repeat` binds `i` over
[from, to). Every template gate MUST itself match a `gates` entry
(schemes do not nest) and is a leaf event; the scheme's error is the sum
of its template gates' charged errors. Scheme ancillae add to the frame
descriptor. A scheme's correctness is the target author's obligation
(suite E: T3 for permutation schemes, suite D for ε > 0).

**Topology.** `topology` is optional. Its `vertices` array orders the
physical vertices; a vertex's index is its address and its `class` MUST
name a declared addressable class. Each edge is an undirected pair of
integer indices `[u,v]`, with `0 ≤ u < v < vertices.length`; edges MUST
be sorted lexicographically, without duplicates or self-loops. Unknown
fields reject. Each live wire occupies one vertex of its resolved
class, distinct from every other simultaneously live wire. The number
of vertices of a class limits occupancy in addition to its capacity.
A native multi-wire gate is realisable only if every pair of its
operand vertices is joined by an edge (including all effective control
wires); single-wire gates need no edge. Target schemes MUST meet this
rule for each native template leaf, including their ancillae. §11
schedules bind these placements to the target hash. Absent `topology`
means all-to-all with the existing class capacities. Routing and
movement costs are deferred to 0.10 under §17 item 15; placement itself
is validated in 0.9.

**TOPOLOGY witness**

```json
{"capabilities":{"classical_step_time":1,"max_parallel":"unbounded","max_predicate_depth":"unbounded","mbu":false,"measurement":{"feedforward_latency":0,"mid_circuit":false}},"classes":{"fast":{"capacity":4,"dirty_rate":1,"qubit_second":1}},"gates":[{"classes":["fast"],"controls":{"max":1,"min":1},"cost":{"time":1},"epsilon":0,"op":"cx"}],"imports":{},"objective":{"weighted":{"time":1}},"ptr_widths_supported":[32],"qram":null,"resources":{"peak_qubits":{"kind":"peak","unit":"qubit"},"qubit_second":{"kind":"occupancy","unit":"qubit_second"},"time":{"kind":"time","unit":"second"}},"schemes":[],"tables":{},"topology":{"edges":[[0,1],[0,2],[1,3],[2,3]],"vertices":[{"class":"fast"},{"class":"fast"},{"class":"fast"},{"class":"fast"}]}}
```

The vertices are the row-major 2×2 grid; its four edges connect
horizontal and vertical neighbors. A CX on vertices 0 and 1 is
realisable; on 0 and 3 it is infeasible under §17 item 15. The canonical
UTF-8 bytes above exclude the code-fence LF.
Target SHA-256: `960cee71e88da141cc6c2b13cab3f28fce1d50c006e67446aadb592e2bfd0882`.

### 8.4 Space budget

The capacity constraint is peak live qubits per class.

## 9. Objects, identity, linking, reversible ABI (object 0.9)

### 9.1 Sections and schemas

Order fixed: `symbols imports functions data devices layout schedule
certificate coverage`; each at most once; unknown sections or fields are
errors. Metadata sections are one JCS line each, an object:

```
  symbols:  { "<local index>": { "name", "kind": "cc"|"r"|"oracle"|"action"|"perm_action"|"lproc"|"data", "signature": <sig>, "interface_hash" } }
  imports:  { "<name>": { "signature": <sig>, "pinned_closure_hash"?: hex } }
  devices:  { "models": { "<dev>": { "version": "0.9" } }, "services": { "<reverse-dns id>": { "version": "…" } } }
  layout:   { "ptr_width", "H", "A_tab", "S_cells", "null_size", "heap_base", "heap_limit_max",
              "globals": { "<name>": { "base", "size", "image": "<data name>" } },
              "interface_regions": { "<oracle>": { "<region>": { "base" } } }, "block_image"?: "<data name>" }
  schedule: §11      certificate: §12      coverage: { "entries": [ §13 ] }
  <sig>:    kind "oracle": { "tier":"q", "kind", "in":[w…], "tgt":w, "fault":F, "mem":[{"A","W","role"}], "args":[types], "abi":"0.9" }
            kind "action"/"perm_action": { "tier":"q", "kind", "mut":[w…], "in":[w…], "mem":[…], "args":[types], "bounds":[…], "abi" }
            kind "lproc": { "tier":"q", "kind", "params":[w…], "result":w, "tokens":["token"…], "args":[types], "mem":[{"A","W","role":"in"}], "abi" }
            kind "cc"/"r": { "tier", "kind", "params":[types], "results":[types], "abi" }
            kind "data": { "tier":"-", "kind", "size", "abi" }
```

Integers that may exceed 2^53 are decimal strings, and a JSON number ≥
2^53 anywhere in metadata is an error. `data` lines are `NAME SP HEX`.
**Encodings.** `types` in `args`, `params` and `results` are the §16.2
type spellings as JSON strings (`"i64"`, `"ptr"`, `"f64"`, `"token"`,
`"qreg[8]"`); `w` in `in`, `tgt`, `mut`, lproc `params` and `result` is
the integer bundle or location width (§16.3). An lproc's `tokens`,
`args` and `mem` arrays are mandatory (empty arrays when absent from the
header); `args` excludes tokens and `mem` has only `in` roles (§3.7).
Array order is header order within each group. A **program-point id**
(§9.2) is the JSON array `[interface_hash, block name, ordinal]`.
Certificate `frame` fields use exactly the typed-record schema of §9.4;
unknown fields are errors there as in every metadata record.
`devices.models` lists every §7.3 model that an `effect` of the object
uses (an `effect` on an unlisted §7.3 model is a load error; a device
outside §7.3 is never listed and faults at run time, §17 item 3; a key
that is not a §7.3 model is an unknown field); `devices.services` lists
every `hostcall` service (§10). An interface region's name (its param
name and its `layout.interface_regions.<oracle>` key) MUST differ from
every symbol and import name of the object, since `@name` in `lift.addr`
denotes either (§9.2).

### 9.2 Identity

An lproc's token, classical and region parameter groups are part of
its JCS signature (§9.1), including their order and types. The header
and every forwarded operand list are canonical function bytes; changing
them changes the envelope and, through references, the caller's closure
hash. Fixed binding instances are derived per oracle (§3.7), not new
symbols or separately hashed bodies; instance rows share the source
row's program-point id and expand once per fixed row instance per step,
while the instance and semantic-call occurrence components in §11
invocation paths and §12.1 context keys distinguish their uses. [deps: ISA-3.7-P5, ISA-3.7-P6, ISA-11.1-P9, ISA-12.1-P11] Entry argument values and token tuples bind invocations through
`params_hash`, not through the lproc's interface hash (this section).
Imports must match all five groups; validation and instance formation
are repeated after linking (§9.3).

**Canonical function bytes**: the text from the function's header line
through its last instruction line, lines joined by LF, no trailing LF.
**Semantic envelope** of a symbol (byte-exact; every part ends with LF):

```
  (1) "isa=" <full version string, e.g. 0.9>
  (2) "ptr_width=" INT
  (3) the JCS signature
  (4) the canonical function bytes (with reference tokens replaced, below); for a data symbol: its data line NAME SP HEX
  (5a) for every data entry in the closure set, sorted by name bytewise:        "data " NAME " " HEX
  (5b) for every layout leaf in the closure set, sorted by JCS path bytes: "layout " <JCS key-component array> " " <JCS value>. [deps: ISA-9.1, ISA-16.1]
  (5c) for an R/CC function, for every device model and service it uses, sorted: "device " <name> " " <JCS entry>
  (6) for every referenced symbol outside the symbol's SCC, in order of first reference: its 64-hex hash
```

**Closure set**, by symbol kind. **Oracle**: **all** `layout` leaves of
the object except `interface_regions` entries of other oracles (every
global's pre-entry affects its allocation faults), its own `interface_
regions.<oracle>.*` leaves, and every data image those leaves name.
**Lproc**: the leaves and images of every global `@g` it names in `lift.
addr`. **Action / permutation action**: no layout leaves; the `@image`
of each `qregion` is an ordinary symbol reference (replaced in part (4),
listed in part (6)). **CC / R function**: all `layout` leaves, every
data image they name, and in (5c) every device model and `hostcall`
service it uses. **Data symbol**: parts (5)–(6) are empty. A layout leaf
is a scalar or an object at the deepest level the schema lists (`globals.
<name>` and `interface_regions.<oracle>.<region>` are leaves; `ptr_
width`, `H`, … are leaves); a scalar's JCS value is its decimal text.
**Globals are data symbols**: every global `g` in `layout.globals` MUST
have `image` = `g` and the object MUST define a `data` symbol named `g`
of that size, so the token `@g` in `lift.addr` is both the global and a
symbol reference (replaced by the data symbol's hash; the global's
`base` and `size` enter through the layout leaf). An `@name` naming an
interface region (in `lift.addr`) is not a symbol reference and is left
unchanged. **Reference replacement**: in parts
(4)–(5) every `@name` symbol-reference token is replaced by `@` followed
by the referenced symbol's 64-hex hash (the symbol's own name in its
header line is a definition, not a reference, and is left unchanged); the **interface hash** uses
interface hashes of defined symbols and `SHA-256(name ‖ 0x00 ‖ JCS
signature)` (hex) for unresolved imports; the **closure hash** uses
closure hashes. **Acyclic and cyclic symbols.** The reference graph
has an edge from a symbol to every symbol it references (parts
(4)–(5)). An **acyclic** symbol — a one-member SCC that does not
reference itself — has hash = SHA-256 of its own envelope (example 1's
`inc8`, example 2's `zero` and `sum_db`); the SCC procedure below applies
to every **cyclic** SCC, including a one-member SCC whose function
references itself (example 2's `sum`). **SCCs**: members ordered by name bytewise; every intra-
SCC `@name` token (parts (4)–(5)) is replaced by `@$<decimal index>`;
intra-SCC references contribute no part-(6) line; `scc_hash` = SHA-256
over the concatenation of the members' envelopes in that order; member
hash = SHA-256 over the ASCII bytes of `<scc_hash hex>:<decimal index>`.
**object_hash** = SHA-256 of the canonical object text with the
`schedule`, `certificate` and `coverage` sections deleted together with
the blank line preceding each. `reference_program_hash` = the oracle's
interface hash. `params_hash` = SHA-256 of the JCS array of classical
parameter values (the typed encoding of §12.3, K13); `schedule_hash` =
SHA-256 of the schedule's JCS bytes.
**Program-point id** = `(symbol interface hash, block name, instruction
ordinal within the block counting nested lines in text order)`.
Executable certificates and caches bind to **closure hashes** (required,
§12).

A layout leaf's path is the JCS array of its original JSON key
components from the layout root to the deepest schema-listed leaf,
so a scalar has a one-component path and an interface-region leaf has
path `["interface_regions","<oracle>","<region>"]`.
[deps: ISA-9.1-P1, ISA-16.1]
Part (5b) emits exactly one line for each structured leaf in the closure
set, including distinct leaves whose dotted renderings coincide, ordered
by the unsigned UTF-8 bytes of that JCS path array, independently of the
leaf values; no dotted rendering is used as a key or an ordering key.
[deps: ISA-9.1-P1, ISA-16.1]
An oracle's own interface-region leaves and entries of other oracles
are selected by equality of the original JSON key under
`interface_regions` to the oracle's name, never by a prefix of a
rendered path; an lproc's global leaves are selected by equality of
the key under `globals` to the global name.
[deps: ISA-3.7-P1, ISA-9.1-P1]

**Envelope boundary lemma** (non-normative).
For a function symbol, §16.2 requires a header beginning `fn `,
`oracle `, `action `, `perm_action ` or `lproc `; following lines are
`regs`, block labels, instructions, or structured closing braces. [deps: ISA-16.2]
No §4 opcode is `data`, `layout` or `device`; a result with one of
those names has ` = ` after the name, which cannot begin any part-(5)
line (a data name, a JSON array, or a device name is required there). [deps: ISA-4.8, ISA-9.1, ISA-16.2]
No canonical function line is a bare 64-hex string, so the first
part-(5)/(6) line ends the function bytes unambiguously. [deps: ISA-4.8, ISA-16.2]
The fixed `data `, `layout `, `device ` prefixes distinguish (5a–c),
and a bare 64-hex line distinguishes (6); JSON strings escape LF. [deps: ISA-9.1, ISA-16.1]
For a data symbol, the signature kind selects exactly one data line
for (4), with (5)–(6) empty, even when the data name itself is `data`,
`layout` or `device`. [deps: ISA-9.1, ISA-16.2]
Together with the fixed first three lines and the injective JCS array
paths, these facts make the envelope parts recoverable without length
prefixes; this is an encoding argument, not a claim of SHA-256 injectivity. [deps: ISA-9.1, ISA-16.1, ISA-16.2, ISA-9.2-P4]

### 9.3 Linking

Imports resolve by name + signature (mismatch: link error; pinned hash:
equality required); resolved closure hashes are recorded in the linked
certificate. Equal-closure-hash duplicates deduplicate; unequal under one
name, or conflicting `devices`/`layout`/`data` entries: link error.
Linking = resolution, validation, certificate composition (§12.3),
rebinding to closure hashes.

### 9.4 Reversible ABI

- CC/R calls: classical frame; callee frames follow the call boundary;
  `uncall` reverses segments (§7.4).
- Region calls: interface bundles and regions passed in declared order,
  caller-owned; callee owns its workspace, returns it clean, local regions
  at image, borrowed bundles returned.
- **Frame descriptor** (certificate): interface slots retain their identities;
  scratch is recorded by class and cleanliness, with widths as expressions
  in inherited control count `c`; retained history is B × frame width for
  the full-history oracle expansion, otherwise the bound library's realised
  history (§17 item 18). Borrowed wires and scheme ancilla frames are
  separate records. Callees `qborrow` only from the caller's designated
  idle set. Shared frames require disjoint live ranges (suite F).

**Frame JSON schema (B5).** The descriptor is the following JCS object;
all five arrays are required, including empty arrays. Every record has
exactly the shown fields; `live_ranges` is optional. Unknown fields at
any depth are errors under §9.1.

```
  { "slots": [ { "id": "<slot identity>", "role": "in"|"tgt"|"fault"|"mut"|"mem_in"|"mem_mut", "class": "<cls>", "width": expr }, … ],
    "scratch": [ { "id": "<frame identity>", "class": "<cls>", "state": "clean"|"dirty", "width": expr }, … ],
    "history": [ { "id": "<frame identity>", "class": "<cls>", "width": expr }, … ],
    "borrow": [ { "id": "<frame identity>", "class": "<cls>", "width": expr }, … ],
    "ancillae": [ { "id": "<frame identity>", "scheme": "<scheme name>", "class": "<cls>", "state": "clean"|"dirty", "width": expr }, … ],
    "live_ranges"?: [ { "frame": "<frame identity>", "start": ordinal, "end": ordinal }, … ] }
```

`slots` lists the source interface in operand order: oracle `in`, `tgt`,
`fault`, then regions; action `mut`, `in`, then regions. Slot identities
are `<group>:<zero-based index within group>` (`in:0`, `tgt:0`,
`fault:0`, `mut:0`, `mem:0`, …); a region uses role `mem_in` or
`mem_mut`. A region slot's width counts all its cells, in address then
bit order; bundle bits are least-significant first. Slot widths MUST
match the signature. Inherited controls are caller-owned and precede
these slots in §11 topology placement; they are not renamed callee
slots. Every class is a resolved target class. Non-slot frame identities
are nonempty producer-chosen strings, distinct from every other record
identity in the descriptor; a dynamic identity additionally includes
the invocation context and the expansion scratch identity (§11.1).
Array order is source interface order for slots, and first allocation
order in the realised expansion for each other array. `scratch` records
per-class, per-cleanliness workspace; `history` records retained history;
`borrow` records dirty requirements; `ancillae` records each scheme's
ancilla frame and its declared state. These categories do not double
count the same wires. No requirements may be omitted because they vanish
for one chosen basis input.

Every `expr` uses §8.1's expression language with **only `c` bound**;
other variables and table lookups (whose indices require `k` or `eps`)
are invalid. At every covered inherited control count the widths MUST
be nonnegative integers; interface widths are positive and signature
fixed. Instantiation substitutes the resolved budget, parameters and
expansion choices into constants before forming these expressions.
Widths describe the actual realisation, including history and scheme
ancillae, and are checked against it. Each `live_ranges` entry names a
non-slot frame identity and a half-open interval `[start,end)` of its
invocation's generated leaf order (including step/phase/path expansion,
§11); `start < end`. A shared frame identity retains its recorded class, state and width
across uses; every use of shared storage MUST be listed and intervals
of the same frame MUST be pairwise disjoint. An omitted list gives no shared-storage permission.

**FRAME witness**

```json
{"ancillae":[{"class":"fast","id":"a","scheme":"controlled-x","state":"clean","width":{"var":"c"}}],"borrow":[{"class":"fast","id":"b","width":1}],"history":[{"class":"fast","id":"h","width":3}],"live_ranges":[{"end":2,"frame":"s","start":0},{"end":4,"frame":"s","start":2}],"scratch":[{"class":"fast","id":"s","state":"clean","width":{"args":[2,{"var":"c"}],"op":"+"}}],"slots":[{"class":"fast","id":"mut:0","role":"mut","width":2},{"class":"fast","id":"in:0","role":"in","width":1}]}
```

At `c = 2`, the slot widths total 3, scratch width is 4, retained
history width is 3, borrow width is 1 and scheme ancilla width is 2.
The two half-open uses of `s` meet at 2 and do not overlap. This is a
schema/arithmetic witness; a real certificate must also bind the
expansion and target defining `controlled-x`. Its canonical UTF-8
bytes exclude the code-fence LF.
Frame SHA-256: `f314eeef24a5a65a9c72f91e062aa3c83556358eaca166e689571700a72083ff`.

  grades it.
- Section 17 lists decisions deferred to implementation with their

  files an issue and the default goes into section 17 first.

## Reproducible finite checks

From the repository root under the environment prefix:

```sh
timeout 300 python3 -I -B -u - <<'PY'
from pathlib import Path
p = Path('docs/notes/2026-11-02-pass2/INDEX.md')
s = p.read_text().split('```python\n', 1)[1].split('\n```', 1)[0]
exec(compile(s, str(p), 'exec'))
PY
```

This code checks exact small mathematical witnesses and actual published
reference interfaces. The arithmetic is not an implementation of any new
certificate verifier or a substitute for its required conformance tests.

```python
from pathlib import Path
from itertools import product
from fractions import Fraction as F
import copy
import hashlib
import json
import os
import re
import subprocess

ROOT = Path('/home/tobias/Projects/bennett-workspace/bennett-isa-spec')
OUT = ROOT / 'docs/notes/2026-11-02-pass2'
SCRATCH = ROOT / '.tmp/sol-contract-pass2'
REF = SCRATCH / 'target/debug/bennett-isa-ref'
PROBE = SCRATCH / 'target/debug/k82-probe'
assert Path.cwd() == ROOT
assert os.environ['TMPDIR'] == str(SCRATCH / 'tmp')
assert os.environ['CARGO_TARGET_DIR'] == str(SCRATCH / 'target')
assert not any(k in os.environ for k in
               ('DISPLAY', 'WAYLAND_DISPLAY', 'DBUS_SESSION_BUS_ADDRESS', 'BROWSER'))

isa = ['0jq', '4ta', 'v2v', '7s0', '95w', '0n5', 'jw7', 'cwq',
       'ysw', '552', 'ojy']
ids = ['b-isa-spec-' + x for x in isa] + [
       'b-dialect-spec-2cg', 'b-dialect-spec-djm']
assert {p.name for p in OUT.iterdir()} == {x + '.md' for x in ids} | {'INDEX.md'}
for ident in ids:
    p = OUT / (ident + '.md')
    s = p.read_text()
    for title in ('Defect', 'North Star', 'Alternatives',
                  'Recommended exact spec diff', 'Hashes', 'Conformance', 'Release'):
        assert title.lower() in s.lower(), (ident, title)
    assert ident in s and not re.search(r'\b(?:TODO|TBD)\b', s)
for p in OUT.glob('*.md'):
    s = p.read_text()
    assert sum(line.startswith('```') for line in s.splitlines()) % 2 == 0, p
    # Remove fenced examples before treating Markdown syntax as links.
    prose = re.sub(r'```[^\n]*\n.*?\n```', '', s, flags=re.S)
    for link in re.findall(r'\[[^\]]+\]\(([^)]+)\)', prose):
        if not link.startswith(('http://', 'https://', '#')):
            assert (p.parent / link.split('#')[0]).exists(), (p, link)
index = (OUT / 'INDEX.md').read_text()
for ident in ids:
    assert index.count('| [' + ident + ']') == 1, ident
print('PASS: 13 per-bead notes and index; required sections, fences and links')

# 0jq: H = M/sqrt(2), so M*M=2I exactly; each H column has two nonzeros.
M = ((1, 1), (1, -1))
assert [[sum(M[i][k]*M[k][j] for k in range(2)) for j in range(2)]
        for i in range(2)] == [[2, 0], [0, 2]]
assert all(sum(M[i][j] != 0 for i in range(2)) == 2 for j in range(2))
# 95w: Bell +/- have norm squared 2 before division by sqrt(2), overlap zero.
plus, minus = [1, 0, 0, 1], [1, 0, 0, -1]
assert sum(a*b for a, b in zip(plus, minus)) == 0
assert sum(a*a for a in plus) == sum(a*a for a in minus) == 2
assert F(1)-F(1) == 0

# 4ta, v2v, cwq: exact basis maps and separate root/child footprints.
for t in range(4):
    assert (t ^ 3) ^ 3 == t
for t, u in product(range(2), repeat=2):
    assert ((t ^ 1) ^ 1, (u ^ 1) ^ 1) == (t, u)
assert (2, 1*2, 1*2, 1, 1) == (2, 2, 2, 1, 1)  # split time, A/B occupancy/peak
child_footprints = [(1, 1, 1), (1, 2, 2)]  # time, occupancy, peak
root = (2, 2*2, 2)
assert root == (2, 4, 2)
assert sum(c[2] for c in child_footprints) != root[2]
assert root[1] + sum(c[1] for c in child_footprints) != root[1]
assert F(1) + F(1,4) == F(5,4) and F(2) + F(1,4) == F(9,4)

# 7s0: a finite model of K76's all-operand stack rule for these CX-only prefixes.
def entry_c(sequence):
    values = {'a': None, 'x': None, 'c': 0}
    stacks = {w: [] for w in values}
    events = []
    for control, target in sequence:  # cx and mcx(1) already normalized to one kind
        identity = (control, target)
        operands = (control, target)
        top = stacks[control][-1] if stacks[control] else None
        if (top is not None and all(stacks[w] and stacks[w][-1] == top
                                   for w in operands)
                and events[top][0] == identity):
            before = events[top][1]
            for w in operands:
                stacks[w].pop()
                values[w] = before[w]
        elif values[control] == 0:
            continue  # disabled: no causal event
        else:
            event = len(events)
            events.append((identity, {w: values[w] for w in operands}))
            for w in operands:
                stacks[w].append(event)
            values[target] = (values[target] ^ values[control]
                              if all(values[w] is not None for w in operands)
                              else None)
    return values['c']
assert entry_c([]) == 0
assert entry_c([('a','c'), ('a','c')]) == 0
assert entry_c([('a','c'), ('x','c'), ('a','c'), ('x','c')]) is None
def frame_ok(c_values):
    return all((c, b ^ c) == (c, b) for c in c_values for b in range(2))
assert frame_ok([0]) and not frame_ok([0, 1])
assert not frame_ok(sorted(set([0] + [1])))  # repeated-visit D_F union cannot be narrowed
entry_products = [(0,0), (1,1)]
assert all(b ^ c ^ d == b for c,d in entry_products for b in range(2))
assert not all(b ^ c ^ d == b for c,d,b in product(range(2), repeat=3))
assert sum(a*b for a, b in zip((1,1), (1,-1))) == 0  # phase on B changes |+>

# 0n5: legal state size versus sample count, without allocating it.
assert (1 << 63) // 8 == (1 << 60)
# jw7: all input/target/fault tuples; F_B's code, not final XOR word.
for budget, expected_faults in [(1, 256), (2, 0)]:
    faults = 0
    for x, target, fault in product(range(4), range(4), range(16)):
        payload, code = (0, 1) if budget == 1 else ((x+1) % 4, 0)
        after = (x, target ^ payload, fault ^ code)
        assert 0 <= after[1] < 4 and 0 <= after[2] < 16
        faults += code != 0
    assert faults == expected_faults
assert 1 ^ 1 == 0 and 1 ^ 0 == 1

# ysw: identity partitions are independent of canonical tuple values.
def partition(handles):
    seen, result = {}, []
    for h in handles:
        if h not in seen:
            seen[h] = len(seen)
