/// Describe an already placed action's physical frame, using the published
/// 0.9-draft B5 records. No generated ordinals are invented for shared storage.
pub fn placed(
    p: &crate::placement::Placed,
    o: &crate::object::Object,
    draft: bool,
) -> Result<Frame> {
    if !draft {
        bail!("frame: explicit --isa 0.9-draft required (§9.4)")
    }
    let name = p
        .request
        .get("region")
        .and_then(Value::as_str)
        .ok_or("frame: placed region binding")?;
    let f = o.function(name).ok_or("frame: missing source function")?;
    let mut starts = BTreeMap::new();
    let mut interface_width = 0;
    for (n, w) in p.source.top_circuit()?.interface_bundles() {
        starts.insert(n, interface_width);
        interface_width += w as usize;
    }
    let mut slots = vec![];
    let groups = if f.kind == crate::object::FnKind::Oracle {
        vec![(0, "in"), (1, "tgt"), (2, "fault"), (3, "mem")]
    } else {
        vec![(0, "mut"), (1, "in"), (2, "mem")]
    };
    for (g, role) in groups {
        for (i, param) in f.groups[g].iter().enumerate() {
            let width = match param.ty {
                crate::object::Type::Wire(w) => w as usize,
                crate::object::Type::Region(a, w) => {
                    (1usize.checked_shl(a).ok_or("host limit: frame region")?)
                        .checked_mul(w as usize)
                        .ok_or("host limit: frame region")?
                }
                _ => bail!("frame: interface type (§9.4)"),
            };
            let offset = *starts
                .get(&param.name)
                .ok_or("frame: source interface mapping")?;
            let class = p.classes.get(offset).ok_or("frame: missing class")?;
            if p.classes[offset..offset + width].iter().any(|k| k != class) {
                bail!("unimplemented: heterogeneous slot class; bennett-circuits-4fa (§9.4)")
            }
            slots.push(crate::certificate::obj([
                ("id", Value::Str(format!("{role}:{i}"))),
                (
                    "role",
                    Value::Str(if role == "mem" {
                        format!("mem_{}", param.role.as_deref().unwrap_or("in"))
                    } else {
                        role.into()
                    }),
                ),
                ("class", Value::Str(class.clone())),
                ("width", Value::Int(width.to_string())),
            ]));
        }
    }
    let mut scratch = vec![];
    let mut history = vec![];
    let mut borrow = vec![];
    let mut ancillae = vec![];
    for q in interface_width..p.classes.len() {

            .placement
            .lifetimes

            ("width".into(), Value::Int("1".into())),

//! CC quantum-register ownership and the reversible handle transitions of §3.3.
use crate::{err, quantum::Wire, Error, QuantumState, Result};
use std::collections::{BTreeMap, BTreeSet};

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
enum Status {
    Live,
    Viewed,
    Consumed,
}
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
struct Handle {
    wires: Vec<Wire>,
    status: Status,
    parent: Option<u64>,
}
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq)]
pub struct QuantumRegisters {
    pub(crate) state: QuantumState,
    handles: BTreeMap<u64, Handle>,
    next: u64,
}
impl Default for QuantumRegisters {
    fn default() -> Self {
        Self::new()
    }
}
#[derive(Clone, Debug)]
enum KernelUndo {
    None,
    Allocation(Vec<Wire>),
    Release(Vec<(usize, Wire)>),
}
#[derive(Clone, Debug)]
pub(crate) struct RegisterUndo {
    handles: Vec<(u64, Option<Handle>)>,
    next: u64,
    wire_next: u64,
    kernel: KernelUndo,
}

impl QuantumRegisters {
    pub fn new() -> Self {
        Self {
            state: QuantumState::new(),
            handles: BTreeMap::new(),
            next: 1,
        }
    }
    pub fn quantum_state(&self) -> &QuantumState {
        &self.state
    }
    pub fn prepare(&mut self, id: u64, bits: &[bool]) -> Result<()> {
        let wires = self.ordinary(id)?.wires.clone();
        if wires.len() != bits.len() {
            return err("§10", "E_ARG: preparation width mismatch");
        }
        let mut check = self.state.clone();
        check.release(&wires)?;
        for (wire, bit) in wires.iter().zip(bits) {
            if *bit {
                self.state.gate("x", &[*wire], None, &[])?;
            }
        }
        Ok(())
    }
    pub fn bundle(&self, id: u64) -> Result<&[Wire]> {
        let h = self
            .handles
            .get(&id)
            .filter(|h| h.status == Status::Live)
            .ok_or_else(|| Error::new(format!("§3.3: qreg {id} is not live")))?;
        Ok(&h.wires)
    }
    fn ordinary(&self, id: u64) -> Result<&Handle> {
        self.bundle(id)?;
        let h = &self.handles[&id];
        if h.parent.is_some() {
            return err(
                "§3.3",
                "a qreg view may only be used by its one region call",
            );
        }
        Ok(h)
    }
    pub(crate) fn idle_bundle(&self, id: u64) -> Result<&[Wire]> {
        Ok(&self.ordinary(id)?.wires)
    }
    fn set_status(&mut self, id: u64, status: Status) -> Result<()> {
        self.handles
        Ok(undo)
    }
    pub fn slice(&mut self, id: u64, lo: usize, len: usize) -> Result<u64> {
        Ok(self.slice_recorded(id, lo, len)?.0)
    }
    pub(crate) fn slice_recorded(
        &mut self,
        id: u64,
        lo: usize,
        len: usize,
    ) -> Result<(u64, RegisterUndo)> {
        let h = self.ordinary(id)?;
        let end = lo
            .checked_add(len)
            .filter(|end| *end <= h.wires.len())
            .ok_or_else(|| Error::new("§3.3: qreg slice out of bounds".into()))?;
        let mut wires = crate::reserved(len, "§3.3", "qreg slice wires")?;
        wires.extend_from_slice(&h.wires[lo..end]);
        let undo = self.record(&[id, self.next]);
        self.set_status(id, Status::Viewed)?;
        let view = self.create(wires, Some(id))?;
        Ok((view, undo))
    }
    pub fn concat(&mut self, a: u64, b: u64) -> Result<u64> {
        Ok(self.concat_recorded(a, b)?.0)
    }
    pub(crate) fn concat_recorded(&mut self, a: u64, b: u64) -> Result<(u64, RegisterUndo)> {
        if a == b {
            return err("§3.3", "qreg concat operands alias");
        }
        let a_wires = &self.ordinary(a)?.wires;
        let b_wires = &self.ordinary(b)?.wires;
        let count = a_wires.len().checked_add(b_wires.len()).ok_or_else(|| {
            Error::host_resource("§3.3: host limit: qreg concat count exceeds usize".into())
        })?;
        let mut wires = crate::reserved(count, "§3.3", "qreg concat wires")?;
        wires.extend_from_slice(a_wires);
        wires.extend_from_slice(b_wires);
        if wires.iter().collect::<BTreeSet<_>>().len() != wires.len() {
            return err("§3.3", "qreg concat wires overlap");
        }
        let undo = self.record(&[a, b, self.next]);
        self.set_status(a, Status::Consumed)?;
        self.set_status(b, Status::Consumed)?;
        let id = self.create(wires, None)?;
        Ok((id, undo))
    }
    pub fn complete_call(&mut self, ids: &[u64]) -> Result<()> {
        self.complete_call_recorded(ids).map(|_| ())
    }
    pub(crate) fn complete_call_recorded(&mut self, ids: &[u64]) -> Result<RegisterUndo> {
        let mut affected = Vec::new();
        let mut wires = BTreeSet::new();
        for id in ids {
            for wire in self.bundle(*id)? {
                if !wires.insert(*wire) {
                    return err("§3.3", "region interface qregs overlap");
                }
            }
            affected.push(*id);
            if let Some(parent) = self.handles[id].parent {
                affected.push(parent);
            }
        }
        let undo = self.record(&affected);
        for id in ids {
            if let Some(parent) = self.handles[id].parent {
                self.set_status(*id, Status::Consumed)?;
                self.set_status(parent, Status::Live)?;
            }
        }
        Ok(undo)
    }
    pub(crate) fn measure(&mut self, id: u64, draw: f64) -> Result<Vec<bool>> {
        if !(0.0..1.0).contains(&draw) {
            return err("§14.2", "measurement draw outside [0,1)");
        }
        let wires = self.ordinary(id)?.wires.clone();
        let branches = self.state.measurement_branches(&wires)?;
        let total = branches
            .iter()
            .fold(crate::exact::Amplitude::zero(), |s, b| s.add(&b.weight));
        let negative_threshold = total.times_f64(draw)?.times_f64(-1.0)?;
        let mut cumulative = crate::exact::Amplitude::zero();
        let mut selected = None;
        for branch in branches {
            seen[h] = len(seen)
        result.append(seen[h])
    return result
def valid_labels(labels):
    return (all(type(x) is int and x >= 0 for x in labels)
            and labels == partition(labels))

assert all(not valid_labels(x) for x in ([1,1], [0,2], [0,-1]))

def jcs(x):
    return json.dumps(x, sort_keys=True, separators=(',', ':'), ensure_ascii=False).encode()

# 2cg: exact primitive request/bump history; production metadata stores are not simulated.
def allocation_trace(production, limit):
    bump, records = 4096, []
    def alloc(n):
        nonlocal bump
        if bump + max(n, 1) > 4096 + limit:
            raise OverflowError
        p = bump
        records.append((p, n))
        bump += max(n, 1)
        return p
    payload, code = None, 0
    try:
        alloc(3)
        if production:
            alloc(0)
        padding = (-bump) % 16
        if padding:
            alloc(padding)
        payload = alloc(16)
        if production:
            alloc(32)
    except OverflowError:
        code = 2
    return payload, bump, records, code
minimal = allocation_trace(False, 32)
production = allocation_trace(True, 64)
assert minimal == (4112, 4128, [(4096,3),(4099,13),(4112,16)], 0)
assert production == (4112, 4160,
                      [(4096,3),(4099,0),(4100,12),(4112,16),(4128,32)], 0)
failed = allocation_trace(True, 32)
assert failed == (4112, 4128, production[2][:-1], 2)
assert 16+32 == 32+16 == 48 and min(16,48) != min(32,48)

def run(argv):
    p = subprocess.run([str(x) for x in argv], cwd=ROOT, text=True,
                       capture_output=True, timeout=120)
    assert p.returncode == 0, (argv, p.returncode, p.stdout, p.stderr)
    return p.stdout

## 17. Decisions deferred to implementation, with defaults

Each item is one an implementer must decide to build; any decision is
conformant provided two implementations agree on the default below until
the item is revised. Items 1–5 are asserted non-semantic.

1. **Binary encoding.** Default: none; §16 text is the only interchange
   form.
2. **Gate-name registry.** Default: the names of §4.4 only; an unknown
   gate name is a load error.
3. **Further device models.** Default: the four of §7.3; an `effect` on
   any other device is `FAULT_EFFECT_UNMODELLED`.
4. **Mechanisation of §14.** Default: none; the reference interpreter is
   the oracle.
5. **Memory layout of the opaque C objects.** Default: opaque; only the
   listed functions touch them.
6. **Measurement-based cleanup** (§6.4): the normative fixup, its
   controlled variant and its certificate kind. Default: unitary cleanup
   (`reverse`); `capabilities.mbu` is `false`.
   *(non-normative witness)* At `bindings.epsilon: "0.1"`, three gates
   lacking exact native matches give N=3 and default (1,30), even if
   one gate has an explicit share; a fourth, exact-native gate takes
   zero and is excluded from N. [deps: ISA-8.1, ISA-8.3, ISA-11-P2]
   With no such inexact gates N=0. [deps: ISA-8.1, ISA-8.3, ISA-11-P2]
   Omitting expansion on a 0.9 object selects the full-library hash in
   item 7, not the single-qxor witness hash. [deps: ISA-11.1-P3, ISA-11.1-P4]
10. **`any`-class resolution** (§11). Default: the first class in JCS
    order with capacity.
11. **Occupancy rate of a class absent from `rate`** (§8.2). Default: 1.
12. **Classical step timing of CC/R instructions and effects** (§8.2).
    Default: `classical_step_time` each, sequential.
13. **Suite sizes** (§15): the exhaustive widths of suite C and the
    sample counts of `sampled` claims. Default: exhaustive for ≤ 16
    interface bits; 2^16 samples above.
14. **Hardware handling of `qreg.free`** (§3.3). Cleanliness of an
    arbitrary state is not testable nondestructively. Default: on
    hardware the release proceeds without a test and its frame is
    marked unverified, so a segment containing it cannot be uncalled
    (§7.4) and a run containing it is not reverse-eligible (§10);
    releasing a register that was not clean is a program error the
    hardware does not detect *(non-normative: physically its wires are
    then discarded as by an unrecorded measurement; this is a program
    error, not a trace-out operation, §10)*. The same holds for
    `bennett_qreg_prepare` and `bennett_qreg_free` on a non-verifying
    executor. A verifying executor tests and records `FAULT_UB_TRAP` on
    failure (§4.9), or returns `E_QREG_NOT_CLEAN` from the host calls.
15. **Routing and movement cost under a topology** (§8.2–§8.3, §11),
    deferred to 0.10. Default: no routing cost is charged and no routing
    is inserted; a schedule that needs a non-adjacent pair is
    `E_SCHEDULE_INFEASIBLE` naming this item. Absent topology remains
    all-to-all. A producer can supply an explicitly routed gate sequence
    using §4.4 `swap` gates; those gates execute and are charged normally.
    The optional topology schema and adjacency validation are decided in
    0.9; only automatic routing and its movement-cost model are deferred.
16. **Exhaustion of an executor's own classical resources in CC/R**
    (activation stack, history storage; §4.9). CC and R carry no budget
    (§5.4 budgets are Q-tier), so where a finite executor runs out is
    its own limit. Default: a terminal fault record `FAULT_BUDGET_HEAP`
    at the instruction that cannot proceed, raised before any mutation
    by that instruction (§4.9; an executor that cannot guarantee this

    fn record(&self, ids: &[u64]) -> RegisterUndo {
        RegisterUndo {
            handles: ids
                .iter()
                .map(|id| (*id, self.handles.get(id).cloned()))
                .collect(),
            next: self.next,
            wire_next: self.state.next_wire_id(),
            kernel: KernelUndo::None,
        }
    }
    fn create(&mut self, wires: Vec<Wire>, parent: Option<u64>) -> Result<u64> {
        let id = self.next;
        let next = id
            .checked_add(1)
            .ok_or_else(|| Error::new("§3.3: qreg identity overflow".into()))?;
        self.handles.insert(
            id,
            Handle {
                wires,
                status: Status::Live,
                parent,
            },
        );
        self.next = next;
        Ok(id)
    }
    pub fn allocate(&mut self, n: usize) -> Result<u64> {
        Ok(self.allocate_recorded(n)?.0)
    }
    pub(crate) fn allocate_recorded(&mut self, n: usize) -> Result<(u64, RegisterUndo)> {
        let mut undo = self.record(&[self.next]);
        let wires = self.state.allocate(n)?;
        undo.kernel = KernelUndo::Allocation(wires.clone());
        let id = self.create(wires, None)?;
        Ok((id, undo))
    }
    pub fn free(&mut self, id: u64) -> Result<()> {
        self.free_recorded(id).map(|_| ())
    }
    pub(crate) fn free_recorded(&mut self, id: u64) -> Result<RegisterUndo> {
        let wires = self.ordinary(id)?.wires.clone();
        let mut undo = self.record(&[id]);
        let positions = wires
            .iter()
            .map(|w| Ok((self.state.position(*w)?, *w)))
            .collect::<Result<Vec<_>>>()?;
        self.state.release(&wires)?;
        self.set_status(id, Status::Consumed)?;
        undo.kernel = KernelUndo::Release(positions);
        Ok(undo)
    }
    pub fn slice(&mut self, id: u64, lo: usize, len: usize) -> Result<u64> {
        Ok(self.slice_recorded(id, lo, len)?.0)
